## Ch. 33's mansion verses, the letters left in doubt: Bharaṇī's and Revatī's minor stars, Jyeṣṭhā's «ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན», Mūla's wall, Aśvinī's numeral (RD176)

Eye reading, 2026-11-06, on the Gser thang reprint (I3CN5029 img. 320, 329, 334) at 3-9x and the Zhol print (WBZ I1KG1710 img. 1066, 1077, 1078, 1084) at 4.5-8x; the crops are in the scratch directory of RD176. The Zhol's image numbers run one to one with the etext's page markers only roughly: p. 314 = img. 1066, p. 322 = 1076, p. 323 = 1077-1078, p. 328 = 1084. The passages themselves stand as their own sections above; their `*Read:*` lines are not raised, because each of the words below is settled on one print only or not at all.

- **Bharaṇī's minor star (wb.2:p314, q631).** On the Zhol print (img. 1066, line 5, near its end) the syllable after the stack «རྒྱ» has the boxy shape of the «པ» of «ཞེས་པ» two syllables on, so «ཕུང་རྒྱབ་ཅན» at 6x. On the reprint (img. 320, the line before the last of the first block) the final of the same syllable is of the family of ང, ན and ད, whose three shapes this print does not keep apart (the final of «ཕུང» and of «ཅན» in the same words look the same), and unlike the print's own བ. The 1996 etext and the Sakya Centre etext (WB2, I1KG12740 p. 452) both give «རྒྱབ». The name is taken as «ཕུང་རྒྱབ་ཅན» (Zhol by eye, two etexts); the reprint's final is a blot of its kind, not a second reading.
- **Revatī's minor star (wb.2:p328, q647).** On the reprint (img. 334, the line of «ཆར་བབས» just above the numerals' block) the last syllable of «ལས་སྣ་དྲོ་?ོད» has crossing strokes at its foot and no round loop, unlike the print's own «ལ» of «ལས» three syllables before, so «ཡོད» at 9x. The Zhol print (img. 1084, line 1) has the same line at 8x but the letter is not told. The Zhol etext's machine text has «ཡོད».
- **Aśvinī's numeral (wb.2:p328, q650).** On the Zhol print (img. 1084, line 4) a small open ring stands above «ཐ» of «ཐ་སྐར»: the Zhol's own interlinear numeral for Aśvinī is the zero «༠», a ring, where the reprint's editors' «༤» is the solid shape of Mṛgaśiras's. So «༤» is the reprint's misprint, and the zero is on the Zhol. Not looked up: the 1996 print's own scan (687 px wide, too small for the numeral).
- **Jyeṣṭhā's «ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན» (wb.2:p323, q649).** Reprint img. 329, line 2 of the full-width lines, at 3x: «ཟམ་འཛུགས་ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན», the ཀ plain, with nothing above it. On the Zhol print (img. 1077, line 5) the line is found, and the syllable has a mark at the top left that may be a ར, not told at 4.5x; the Zhol etext's machine text reads «རྐལེན». The words are therefore «ཆུ་རྐ་ལེན» (taking, that is digging, a water channel, beside «ཟམ་འཛུགས», setting up a bridge) if the ར is there, and a lost ར on the reprint at the worst; no other sense for «ཆུ་ཀ» was found.
- **Mūla's wall (wb.2:p323, q646).** Reprint img. 329: the line ends «བང་བ» and the next begins with a boxy «བ», an angular «བ» of the prefix and a stack with the vowel ི on ཙ over ག, at 5x: «བརྩིག». An «འཛིག» would put a round «འ» before the stack; none is there. The Zhol print (img. 1078, line 3) at 7x shows the same sequence, a «བ» before a stack with ི, read «བརྩིག» (the Zhol agrees, it is not a separate proof, the glyphs being small).

Not read: q609 (p. 305's «དབོ»), q630 («རྗོན» on pp. 317 and 323), q648 («རུས་མཚན», p. 322; Zhol img. 1076 line 2 found, 129 of 288 characters in) and q639 (the wavy marks of p. 517): time.

### Open

Question: Is the reprint's final in Bharaṇī's minor star a ང printed for བ, or is there a Gser thang block that reads «རྒྱང»? (Both Zhol and the two etexts have བ.)

### Searched and not found

- «ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན» and «ཆུ་ཀ» as a word: no hit in the Zhol etext (img. 1030-1110) except «རྐལེན» in this line; no lexicon consulted.
- The 1996 print's own scan for Aśvinī's numeral: 687 px wide, not legible.

### Second part (RD176b, 2026-11-06)

The Zhol print at 4.5-8x (WBZ img. 1076; img. 1054-1055 and 804-805 looked at whole) and witnesses found through BDRC's full-text search (`bdrc_search.py`, exact phrases, read from the etexts' own snippets).

- **The fifth meeting, «བྲ་ཉེ་དབོ» (wb.2:p305, q609).** BDRC's search for «སྤེན་པ་བྲ་ཉེ» (13 hits) brings five other works that state the same five meetings, each as three things meeting (date class, planet, mansion) with one vowel. In every one Saturn's mansion is Bharaṇī alone and nothing follows it that could be a second mansion. Pema Karpo (MW10736, Darjeeling reprint): «རྫོགས་པ་སྤེན་པ་བྲ་ཉེ་གསུམ་འཛོམ་པ་ལ་ལྀ་ཡིག་ཐོག་མའི་མིང་ཅན་འཆི» (the vowel being «ལྀ»). Karmapa III (MW30541): «རྫོགས་པའི་སྤེན་པ་བྲ་ཉེ་རྣམས། འཛོམ་ཚེ་ལི་མིང་ཉོན་མོངས་འགྱུར»; Tshur phu's «Zhal lung» (MW3CN2232): «རྫོགས་པ་སྤེན་པ་བྲ་ཉེ་ལྀ». The two WB etexts (1996 and Gser thang) read «བྲ་ཉེ་དབོ». These are Wylie snippets of BDRC's search page, matched on the sound, and the Tibetan here is my transcription of them: the point is the structure. By it «དབོ» stands where the other four meetings put the vowel («མོན་དྲེ་ཨེ», «སྐག»+«ཨུ») and WB's next line says «ཨོ་ཡི་མིང་ཅན»: «དབོ» is the vowel O's slot, not Uttaraphalgunī. The other works end in a different vowel (a vocalic L, «ལྀ»), which is a difference of tradition; WB's own list (A, O, E, U, I) has O fifth. The Zhol line itself was not found on img. 1054-1055 at 1.5x.
- **«རུས་མཚན» (wb.2:p322, q648).** The Zhol (img. 1076, line 2) at 4-5x: «རུས་མཚན་དབང་པོ་མེ་ལྷ་ལྷ»: «མཚན» is printed there too, with ཚ, so it is no misprint of the reprint. What the words are is not settled by the print: «དབང་པོ་མེ་ལྷ» reads best as two names, Indra and Agni (དབང་པོ, མེ་ལྷ), the pair that Viśākhā's deity is in the Indian lists, but no published source for that pair is in the pool, so the English keeps the query; «རུས་མཚན» then stands alone as "the clan's name", with the name itself missing from the line.
- **Mūla's wall (q646).** The Namkha Senge's SY etext (I1PD30933 etext p. 384, «Skar yig»), which restates the verses in prose, has for Mūla «སྒོ་འཛུགས་བང་བརྩིག» (printed «བང་ བརྩིག»): «བརྩིག» is written there, so with the reprint at 5x and the Zhol at 7x all three agree. q646 answered.
- **Jyeṣṭhā's «ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན» (q649).** The same SY etext (p. 384) has «ཟམ་འཛུགས་བ་ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན་བ»: a plain ཀ in a work independent of WB's blocks. So the reprint's plain ཀ is the tradition's spelling and not a lost ར; the sense of «ཆུ་ཀ» is not known (no lexicon consulted); «ཆུ་རྐ་ལེན» "taking a channel" stays a guess.
- **Revatī's and Bharaṇī's minor stars in SY.** SY (the same etext's pp. 381-386, OCR) has «སྐར་ཆུང་ལས་སྣ་དྲོ་ལོད་ཅེས» for Revatī and «སྐར་ཆུང་རྒྱུབ་ཅན» for Bharaṇī (ུ for the vowel, the final བ). For q631 that is a third witness to བ; for q647 an OCR reading «ལོད», against the reprint's ཡ at 9x: the two are not reconciled, the eye reading stays a lean.
- **p. 517's wavy marks (q639).** Zhol img. 804-805 looked at whole: there are small dark flourishes standing over some words of the lines (img. 804, lines 1 and 2, and again on img. 805), darker than the text; whether they are carved or inked was not told at this size and no count over img. 782-806 was made.
- **«རྗོན» (q630).** No result: see below.

### Open (second part)

Question: Which published list names Viśākhā's deities as Indra and Agni (དབང་པོ་མེ་ལྷ), so that «རུས་མཚན» in WB's verse can be read as a clan name left blank?

- BDRC exact-phrase search, 2026-11-06: «རྗོན་པ་ཅན», «ལྷ་ནི་རྗོན་པ་ཅན», «རྗོན་པ་ཅན་ཞེས», «རྗོན་པ་ཅན་དང», «རྗོན་རྐུ», «སྣུབས་ལྷ་རྗོན»: no hit anywhere. SY (I1PD30933) has no deity or «རྗོན» for Mūla or Puṣya.
- The Zhol line of p. 305's fifth meeting: not located on img. 1054-1055.

### Third part (RD176c, 2026-11-07)

By eye on the Zhol print's img. 1055 whole at 2x, and from the pool's own verse lists; no model, no new etext.

- **«དབང་པོ་མེ་ལྷ» (wb.2:p322, q648): the sense settled.** The pool's own verses use both words alone: Jyeṣṭhā's «ལྷ་དབང་པོ་དང་རུས་ལེགས་བྱེད» (deity Indra), Kṛttikā's «རྗེ་རིགས་མེ་ལྷ» and Hasta's «རུས་ནི་འོད་སྲུངས་ལྷ་མེ་ལྷ» (deity Agni). So Viśākhā's two deities are Indra and Agni, as the second part guessed, and the earlier query to an outside list is not needed for the sense. What stays open is the clan: «རུས་མཚན» has no name after it on either print. [claims/wb-p322-visakha-deities-indra-agni.md](../../claims/wb-p322-visakha-deities-indra-agni.md).
- **Mūla's «རྗོན» (q630) and Jyeṣṭhā's «ཆུ་ཀ་ལེན» (q649).** The pool's lists have no second occurrence of either: «རྗོན» stands only in Puṣya's avoid list and as Mūla's deity, «ཆུ་ཀ» only in Jyeṣṭhā's good list; the other water works of the verses (ཆུ་རགས, ཆུ་བོ་བསྒྱུར, ཆུ་བརྒལ, ཆུ་ལས) give no help. Still a dead end for «རྗོན» (the deity), and for «ཆུ་ཀ» a plain ཀ in three witnesses with the sense of a channel only a guess.
- **The fifth meeting on the Zhol (q609).** Img. 1055 shows seven long lines with coloured marks; the line with «བྲ་ཉེ» was not found by eye at this size, so the Zhol's own reading of «དབོ» is still not seen. The narrowing of the second part stands.
- **Not reached by eye:** q639's count of the wavy marks over img. 782-806 (twenty-five images), q647's Zhol letter at greater enlargement, q650's 1996 numeral. No `*Read:*` line is raised by this part.

By eye on the Zhol print (WBZ, I1KG1710) img. 1084 and 804 at 1x and 2x crops (the card was not used: the Zhol's hand is cursive and a reader's output on it would be the stack-guessing the earlier parts already met); the crops are in the scratch directory of RD176.

The marks that stand over the first lines (above lines 1 and 2, small dark flourishes of a few strokes) are in ink like the text, drawn above the line after the writing; the large grey streaks across lines 4 and 5 and the stains below the frame are damp or wear, not marks. So on this copy the marks are inked by hand, not carved, and cannot be counted as a printed feature. The count against Mipham's 10, 24, 9, 20, 13, 8, 27, 17 and 24 over img. 782-806 was not made: twenty-five images of a hand whose small flourishes vary, and one of them looked at.

### Sixth part (RD176f, 2026-11-08)

By eye, no model: the 1996 print's own scan (I1KG12907, 703 px wide, img. 336 = printed p. 328 and img. 338 = p. 330, cut at 7-14x: it is legible, the earlier parts' "too small" was wrong), the 1996 etext (IE0OPI51524892, `[p336]` = printed p. 328), the Zhol print (img. 1084, line 4, 5x) and the Taikhang reproduction (I5878 img. 466, 8280 px, its line breaks the Zhol's). The crops are in the scratch directory of RD176.

- **Aśvinī's numeral (wb.2:p328, q650): answered.** In the class «ཕྱོགས་སྡེབས་མི་བརྟན་འགྱུར་བ» the Zhol and the Taikhang have a small open ring over «ཐ» of «ཐ་སྐར», after the figures of Puṣya, Hasta and Revatī; the 1996 print (img. 336) has a solid hooked sign there, identical in shape to its own «མགོ(༤)» on img. 338, and its etext transcribes «ཐ་སྐར(༤)»; the same print gives «ཐ་སྐར(༠)» with a ring on img. 338 (p. 330), and the etext's later lists read ༠ (once «ཁ»). So «༤» is shared by the 1996 print and the Gser thang reprint and is a slip of that line; the two Lhasa-family prints have the zero. [claims/wb-p328-aswini-numeral-zero.md](../../claims/wb-p328-aswini-numeral-zero.md).
- **Revatī's minor star (wb.2:p328, q647): still a lean.** The 1996 etext has «ལས་སྣ་དྲོ་ལོད» (like SY's OCR); on the 1996 scan (img. 336, line 12) the last syllable before «ཅེས» has a vowel sign ོ over a base that is open at the top, nearer the ཡ of the reprint's 9x reading than the closed loop of the «ལ» of «ལས» two syllables before, but at 9x of a 703 px page the two are not told apart with certainty. The Taikhang line was looked for on img. 466 (rows 2-3) and not identified in its hand. No `*Read:*` raised.
- **Not reached:** q609's Zhol line (Taikhang not searched for it), q630, q631's reprint final, q639's count, q646, q648, q649: no new reading; their states stand.

### Open (sixth part)

Nothing new.

### Searched and not found (sixth part)

- Taikhang I5878 img. 466: Revatī's «ལས་སྣ་དྲོ» line: not identified among the rows at 0.6-0.8x.

## The meanings left open in ch. 33 and the notes: the departure's singled-out lords, the Mara days' images, the six fang-holders, «ཆུབ», the illness examinations' elements, the secret obstacle, the hand reckoning (etext and commentaries, no card) (RD177)

Read 2026-11-06 from BDRC's etexts and the pool's passages, no model run and no scan opened (RD177): twelve questions put to the commentaries on the same passages: Mipham's notes (WB vol. 2, pp. 474–541), the Moonbeams' supplement in KD vol. 3 (the Garland of Stars and the Description of the *dbyangs 'char*), Lha mo tshul khrims's manual (LT), the *Garland of Blue Utpalas* (UT), Zhechen Gyaltsab's *Lotus Pond* (ZG) and the Bhutan collection's digest (I1KG11905, etext only). The etexts' page markers are image numbers (WB vol. 1: printed page = image − 10; vol. 2: image − 8; KD vol. 3: etext − 20; UT: etext − 22; LT: etext − 8). Pages looked at: WB vol. 1, pp. 317, 375, 390, 395, 417–419 (etext p. 327, 385, 400, 405, 427–429); vol. 2, pp. 305–306, 335–336, 341–342, 501, 517, 538–539 (etext pp. 313–314, 343–344, 349–350, 509, 525, 546–547), p. 531 and p. 480 of the notes (etext pp. 539, 488), and WB's pp. 46, 50–51, 70, 88–89, 96–97, 111–112, 162, 370 (etext pp. 54, 58–59, 78, 96–97, 104–105, 119–120, 170, 378). WB's own words were taken from the passages already read on the scan; **everything below from a commentary is the etext's reading, state *etext*, and builds nothing until a scan is read.** Seven new passages: [kd.3:p732](../kd/kd.md#kd-3-p732), [kd.3:p256-258](../kd/kd.md#kd-3-p256-258), [wb.2:p531](ch35.md#wb-2-p531), [wb.2:p480](ch35.md#wb-2-p480), [ut:p234](../ut/ut.md#ut-p234), [lt:p43b](../lt/lt.md#lt-p43b), [ut:p302-304](../ut/ut.md#ut-p302-304); ten claims.

### The departure's singled-out lords (q605): answered as far as the sources go

The lists of WB's own pp. 96 and 370 place them: the Thé-se (ཐེ་སེ), Thé-khyim (ཐེ་ཁྱིམ), Se-byi (སེ་བྱི) and the watchman (བྱ་ར) among the year's seventeen kings and ministers; Tenma (བསྟན་མ) in the month's eighteen; the Hal-dog (ཧལ་ཁྱི) in the year's and the month's and in the date's own run; the Golden and the Turquoise Treasury (གསེར་མཛོད, གཡུ་མཛོད) the day's and the hour's gods. Bar and remedy are divided by the verse itself («ས་བདག་གནས་དང་རྒྱུ་ཕྱོགས་འཛེམ… ཕྲད་ན་སྒྲིབ་བཅོས་སོ་སོར་སྦྱར»): seat and course shunned, the remedy when met; Mipham's note (p. 531, [wb.2:p531](ch35.md#wb-2-p531)) sends the bride's examination back to the text, the *Garland* repeats the verse ([ut:p234](../ut/ut.md#ut-p234)) and calls the Hal-dog the earth lord's sky dog (ས་བདག་གནམ་ཁྱི་ཧལ་ཁྱི་ཡིན). No commentary grades the six or speaks of a day on which one stands. [claims/departure-singled-out-lords-by-time.md](../../claims/departure-singled-out-lords-by-time.md).

### The Mara days' images (q610): answered

The Moonbeams' supplement names each day's pair, which the tables of p. 341 repeat: Moon with Uttaraphalgunī (11), the iron speckled bird, the nun's day; Mars with Āśleṣā (8), the axe and the goat-heads pressed down, the general's; Mercury with Pūrvāṣāḍhā (19), the empty measure, the barley-measurer's; Saturn with Śatabhiṣaj (23), the golden chain, the horse-dealer's ([kd.3:p732](../kd/kd.md#kd-3-p732): «དེ་བཞིན་ཟླ་དབོ་མོ་བཙུན་དང་། མིག་དམར་སྐག་འཛོམ་དམག་དཔོན་ཏེ། ལྷག་པ་ཆུ་སྟོད་ནས་འཇལ་དང་། … སྤེན་པ་མོན་གྲུར་རྟ་ཚོང་སྟེ»). The images are the day's persons and attributes, not the mansions' shapes; only Jupiter's «མགོ» and Saturn's «སྒྲོག» carry the mansion's own name. [claims/mara-day-images-and-mansions.md](../../claims/mara-day-images-and-mansions.md).

### The six fang-holders (q611): answered

A KD commentary on the *dbyangs 'char* counts them from the birth mansion: the birth place, the work place (tenth mansion), Rnam 'gyes (sixteenth foot), Rgya can (eighteenth foot), Pinaśaka (twenty-third) and Yid can (twenty-fifth); Ananta (nineteenth foot) is added and is not one of the six ([kd.3:p256-258](../kd/kd.md#kd-3-p256-258): «ཞིང་དྲུག་འཛིན་ལ་མ་གཏོགས་སོ»). WB's seven heads are these seven in this order. [claims/fang-holders-six-and-ananta.md](../../claims/fang-holders-six-and-ananta.md).

### «ཆུབ» of the Chub day (q612): a dead end

No commentary glosses the word. The Bhutan digest (etext p. 421) lists «ཆུབ་ཉི་བདུན» "the seven Chub days", Jupiter on Pūrvabhādrapadā like the Paired, good for nearly every good work, with no word on the name; the Moonbeams' supplement ([kd.3:p732](../kd/kd.md#kd-3-p732)) names the Mara days only; LT, UT and ZG do not have the combination. The word stands as RD157 read it ([claims/wb-p335-chub-nyi.md](../../claims/wb-p335-chub-nyi.md)).

### The illness examinations' elements (q592): narrowed

Each examination's element is a relation of the one reckoned's own element: the enemy's tiger (2nd), the mother's (3rd), the friend's (5th), the enemy's, the friend's, the own and the mother's or son's hares (6th–9th), the son's tiger (12th), the mother's monkey (13th); the 1st, 10th, 11th, 14th, 15th and 16th start from the sky year ([lt:p43b](../lt/lt.md#lt-p43b); the Moonbeams' fixed signs are the same relations for a person of wood). It is not the element of the aspect examined: the enemy starts the king's seat and the demon's thief, the friend the spouse and the karmic ogre. The 4th differs: WB's «དབང་ཐང་མཐུན་པ» (agreeing in power), LT's son. [claims/illness-starting-points-by-relation.md](../../claims/illness-starting-points-by-relation.md).

### The secret obstacle (q513): narrowed

By the generating cycle li is the mother of the earth trigrams (khon, gin) and kham of the wood ones (zin, zon), as the manuals have it for the tiger and hare. The line of p. 390 gives li to the thief of one's own birth, kham to the son's, and for the daughter «ཚོར་བ་དཀའ»; why, no text says. [claims/secret-obstacle-ma-spar-by-cycle.md](../../claims/secret-obstacle-ma-spar-by-cycle.md).

### The trigram remedies' fourteen and fifteen (q576): not decided

WB p. 51 says «སྤར་བཅོས་ལ་བཅུ་བཞི» ([wb.2:p517](notes-4.md#wb-2-p517) against the root's line, etext p. 59); the six items of the notes' clause sum to fourteen with the eight; no commentary counts them. [claims/notes-trigram-remedies-fourteen-or-fifteen.md](../../claims/notes-trigram-remedies-fourteen-or-fifteen.md).

### «རྒྱུག» in «སྤུད་པ་དགུ་རྒྱུག་དྲུག» (q632): narrowed

The notes' words follow WB's p. 51 «རྒྱུ་ཡི་སྤུད་དྲུག་སྨེ་བར་དགུ» ("the six *spud* of the going, the mewa's nine"), so «རྒྱུག» is «རྒྱུ་ཡི»; «སྤུད་པ་དགུ» is the rite of p. 111; «ཀུ་ཀུང་ལ་གསུམ» is the three graves of p. 112, or the *Garland*'s three strikes ([ut:p302-304](../ut/ut.md#ut-p302-304)). What a *spud* is, no text gives. [claims/notes-spud-pa-rgyug-is-wb-rgyu.md](../../claims/notes-spud-pa-rgyug-is-wb-rgyu.md).

### «གསུམ» in the labels (q640): narrowed

Not a count of mewas of one colour except for the whites and the reds; the Moonbeams' table of pebbles per mewa (green four: three; white eight: three; red nine: two) fits the labels of p. 70 and the notes as a count of pebbles. [claims/mewa-labels-colour-count.md](../../claims/mewa-labels-colour-count.md).

### The kung of «ཐུན་ཀུང» (q652): a dead end with one lead

Not found in any commentary; the lead is «གུང» (noon) as in the notes' «གུང་གཉིས་ཟེར་གཉིས». [claims/hand-reckoning-kung-a-lead.md](../../claims/hand-reckoning-kung-a-lead.md).

### The *do gcig* (q653): repeats questions 350 and 385

Mipham's note on the birth reckoning's results is the one gloss: the *do gcig* is the trigram fallen on, on top of the born trigram, being reached ([wb.2:p480](ch35.md#wb-2-p480): «སྐྱེས་སྤར་སྟེང་བབ་སྤར་ཕྱིན་པ»). No place defines it further. [claims/do-gcig-glossed-once-in-the-notes.md](../../claims/do-gcig-glossed-once-in-the-notes.md).

### Open

- Question: The line of WB vol. 1, p. 390 gives the thief secret obstacle «སྤར་སྟེང་སླེབ་དང་མ་སྤར་ལི», the son's «མ་སྤར་ཁམ» and the daughter's «ཚོར་བ་དཀའ»: why does the first take the mother's trigram li and the son's kham, when by the generating cycle li is the mother of khon and gin and kham of zin and zon only?
- Question: In the notes' hand reckoning (WB vol. 2, pp. 538–539), is the kung (ཀུང) of «ཐུན་ཀུང» the noon-division «གུང» that follows the watch (ཐུན)?
- Question: Does the notes' «བཅོ་ལྔའོ» (vol. 2, p. 517, l. 8) count the smith's paper-roll and its remedy as two, so that "six each" means seven, or is it a slip for the fourteen of WB's p. 51?
- Question: Are «ནག་གཉིས», «ལྗང་གསུམ» and «སེར་གསུམ» of WB vol. 2, p. 70 (and the notes' p. 501) the pebbles laid for the black, green and yellow mewas, as the red two and the white three are in the Moonbeams' table, and which mewa does each name?
- Question: What is a *spud* in «རྒྱ་ཡི་སྤུད་པ་དགུ» (vol. 2, p. 111) and «རྒྱུ་ཡི་སྤུད་དྲུག» (p. 51)?
- Question: The Bhutan digest lists the seven Chub days (ཆུབ་ཉི་བདུན) after the Increasing days without a word on the name; is «ཆུབ» a mansion-pair term of the same kind as «འགྲུབ», «འཕེལ» and «མཐུན» before it?

### Searched and not found

- «ཆུབ་ཉི», 2026-11-06: the 1996 etext of WB vol. 1 and 2, the KD vol. 3 etext (I4717), LT (I1KG21886), UT (I4767), ZG (I4PD2444) and the Bhutan collection (I1KG11905): the combination stands in WB (p. 335 and the tables of pp. 341–342, etext pp. 343, 349) and in the Bhutan digest (etext p. 421) only; none glosses it.
- «ཐུན་ཀུང», «ཐུན་ཀླུང», «ཐུན་ཀུངས», «ཐུན་གུང»: the same etexts: no hit but WB's own line (the 1996 etext has «ཐུན་ཀཱང», p. 547) and an unrelated «ལོ་མཐུན་ཀླུང་མཐུན» (KD etext p. 607, WB etext p. 167).
- «དོ་གཅིག»: the same etexts besides WB and the notes: no definition.
- «རྒྱུག་དྲུག», «སྤུད་པ་དགུ» as a gloss: the same etexts: none; Steinert's dictionaries were not at hand in this reading (the clone is 4.5 GB; the earlier searches of RD96 stand).
- The departure's seven lords with a day or a grade, in LT, UT, KD vol. 3 and the Bhutan digest: the verse, the sky-dog line and the avoidance lists are all there is; no text says "of the date".


## Ch. 33's machine passages against both readers on the reprint (RD158, 2026-11-07)

Both readers (Yigdzin-1 and MITRA, the card, run now through the slot lock) were put over the Gser thang reprint (I3CN5029, image = page + 6) for img. 364–365 (pp. 358–359), 368–369 (pp. 362–363), 372–374 (pp. 366–368) and 375 (p. 369), and each passage's syllables were aligned against each reader. Below are the places where **both readers agree with each other and differ from the passage as it stands** — the passage's own machine text is then the likelier error, but the two readers share a leaning to the common spelling and agree on hand-written-looking stacks, so none of these is taken as read: the passage is **not** changed for them, and none is raised to scan. Seen on the scan at 3×: wb.2:p362-363, the banner verb of «དར་འཕྱར་བ» (line 2 of the entry at the foot of p. 362) is «འཕྱར» (to hoist, a banner), not «འཕྱུར»; both readers give «འཕྱར» at every one of the places where the passage had «འཕྱུར», so all of them are set right (five in wb.2:p362-363, three in wb.2:p366-368, six in wb.2:p369b; each other place of those passages not touched).

Where the passage and both readers disagree (the passage's reading first):

- wb.2:p358-359: «སྤྲ» → «སྒྲ», «བཙེམ» → «བཅོམ», «ཆོ» → «ཚེ», «དད» → «ངད», «སྒྱེད» → «སྐྱེད», «འབྲུ» → «འཁྲུ», «ཟློག» → «རློག», «ཚེག» → «ཆོག», «ཕུག» → «ཕྲུག», «ནོ» → «ཞོ», «སྤྱོ» → «སྨྱོ», «རྩུབ» → «ཐུབ»
- wb.2:p362-363: «དུར» → «ངུར»
- wb.2:p366-368: «སྒྲ» → «སྒ», «སྲུང» → «སྲང», «སྦྲང» → «སྤང», «ཕྱུར» → «ཕྱར», «རུ» → «རུཾ», «རུ» → «རུཾ», «རུ» → «རུཾ», «རུ» → «རུཾ», «ཚང» → «ཚོང», «རྒྱུ» → «རྐྱ»
- wb.2:p369b: «སྒོར» → «སྒོ», «བདུད» → «བཏུད», «ཚ» → «མྱ», «སི» → «སྲི», «ཀུང» → «ཀྱང», «ཀུང» → «ཀྱང», «དར» → «དུར», «ཀུང» → «ཀྱང», «འདོན» → «འཛིན», «ཀུང» → «ཀྱང», «ཚེས» → «ཆོས», «ཆེན» → «ཅེན», «ཁུར» → «འུར», «སྟབ» → «སྲབ», «སྒ» → «སྦ», «ཁྲམ» → «ཁྲོམ», «ཁྱོད» → «ཁྱེད»

Not read by eye, so not decided: every item above except the banner verb; the many places where only one reader differs from the passage; the readings of pp. 358–359 not aligned for lack of the second page's syllable breaks in Yigdzin-1's file (img. 365: the letters are spaced out, so the alignment lost about forty syllables of the lower half). The Zhol print (WBZ, I1KG1710, img. 1108–1190 fetched into the shared cache; each leaf is five long lines of a hand-written-looking face, one leaf holding about a page and a half of the reprint, so the leaf-to-page map is not one to one) was fetched and looked at, and no line of it was compared. wb.2:p358-359, p362-363, p366-368 and p369b stay **machine**.

### The Zhol print for «བཙེམ»/«བཅོམ» (RD158, second run, 2026-11-07)

The reprint being too coarse for vowel signs at 3–4×, the Zhol print (WBZ, I1KG1710) was used: Yigdzin-1 over img. 1104–1109 and 1111–1113 (scratch only) put the Monkey day's verse («སྤྲེལ་ལ་གོས་གསར་…») on **img. 1112, line 2** of its five lines, a little right of the middle (about 0.56 of the line's width), the page's first verse after the Sheep day's; the leaf holds the end of the Sheep day and the Monkey day's first lines. The word was cut with `scans.py line` at 6× and 10× (band of the line's own ink rows). Seen: the second syllable of the line's second half-verse carries a **naro (ོ, the o sign, a flourish rising to the right above the head letter), not a gigu (ེ)**, so «བཙེམ» of wb.2:p358-359 is wrong in its vowel and the machine readers' «ོ» is right; the head letter after the ba (ཅ or ཙ) and the first syllable's stack (སྤྲ, སྒྲ or སྦྲ; Yigdzin-1 on the Zhol gives «སྦྲ་བཙོ་མ») were **not plain** at 10× on this hand-written face, so the passage is **not changed**: «བཙེམ», «སྤྲ» stay, and the doubt is now exactly: vowel o (seen on the Zhol), ca/tsa and the stack open. The Taikhang print (I5878) is in the shared cache only up to img. 528 (img. 10–528 of 356 files, none yet for pp. 358–359 by the 0.8 × page rule), so it was not reached. The Dog day's verses on the same leaf (img. 1112, the row right of the drawing of the dog: «སྒྱེད»/«སྐྱེད», «འབྲུ»/«འཁྲུ») were cut at 6× as well: on this running hand the head letters ག/ཀ and བ/ཁ were not told apart, so nothing is settled there. The other places of the list above were not looked at in this run.

### Open

Nothing new; the questions of the section above stand.

### Searched and not found

Nothing was searched for outside the prints and the two readers.
